{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_bdrk_01948J2zSg7ixRFofb2Gu6TR","type":"tool_result","content":"README.md\nassets\nbuildspec.yml\nbun.lock\ncloudflare\ncontracts\ndocs\nhooks\nhooks.json\nissue-205-fixed.png\nnode_modules\noverlay\noverlay-release.json\npackage.json\npackages\nscripts\nskills\nsrc\ntauri\ntsconfig.json\n---\n# CLI/browser OAuth handoff — API contract\n\nThis pins down the parts of the browser OAuth handoff (instruction.md) that\nare exercised by direct, white-box tests against exported functions and JSON\nerror/success bodies, rather than by end-to-end behavior alone. Anything not\ncovered here is an implementation choice.\n\n## `src/auth.ts`\n\n### `startOAuthCallbackServer(port)`\n\nReturns **synchronously** (not a `Promise`) an object of this shape:\n\n```ts\ninterface OAuthCallbackServer {\n  readonly port: number;   // may change from the requested port (see fallback below)\n  ready: Promise<void>;    // resolves once the socket is bound and accepting requests\n  token: Promise<string>;  // resolves with the raw token string, or rejects on error/cancel\n  close(): void;\n}\n```\n\nCallers do `const callback = startOAuthCallbackServer(0); await callback.ready;`\n— i.e. the handle itself is available before the socket is guaranteed to be\nbound; only `ready` tells you binding finished (and, on a fallback, that\n`port` has its final value).\n\nBehavior:\n\n- Binds to `127.0.0.1` on `port`. If that port is already taken, retries once\n  on an OS-assigned free port (`port` reflects the port actually bound).\n- Serves `GET /auth/ping` → `200 ok`.\n- Serves `GET /auth/callback`, reading `token`, `state`, `error`, and\n  `error_description` from the query string with a parser that treats a\n  literal `?` the same as `&` (so `?state=<nonce>?token=<value>` yields\n  `token=<value>`, matching the redirect quirk some hosted login pages have).\n- `token` resolves with the **raw, unverified** token string exactly as\n  received in the query. This function does not call the auth backend and\n  does not check `state` against anything: unlike the desktop overlay's\n  equivalent Rust listener (`tauri/src/main.rs`, `handle_callback_connection`),\n  which is handed an `expected_state` nonce it generated itself,\n  `startOAuthCallbackServer` takes only a `port` — it has no nonce to compare\n  against, so any `state` value is accepted and ignored at this layer.\n  Verifying the token against the auth backend (if a caller wants that) is a\n  separate, higher-level concern layered on top of the raw listener, not part\n  of this function.\n- `?error=access_denied` → `400`, body contains \"cancelled\" (case-insensitive),\n  and `token` rejects with an `Error` matching `/cancelled/i`.\n  Any other `?error=...` → `400`, using `error_description` (HTML-escaped in\n  the body) when present, and `token` rejects with an `Error` whose message\n  contains the raw, un-escaped description.\n- Every `/auth/callback` response sets `Cache-Control: no-store`.\n\n## `cloudflare/auth-worker/src/index.ts`\n\n### `GET /cli/handoff`\n\n- Validate `next` first: it must parse as a URL with `protocol === \"http:\"`\n  and `hostname` of `127.0.0.1` or `localhost`. Reject anything else with\n  `400` before touching the session.\n- Decide \"signed in\" from `auth.api.getSession({ headers: request.headers })`\n  — treat the browser as signed in when the result has a `user`. Read the\n  literal token value to attach to `next` from the request's `Cookie` header,\n  specifically the `better-auth.session_token` cookie (better-auth's default\n  cookie name) — not from a field on the object `getSession` returns, since\n  that object's shape (e.g. whether it nests a raw `token`) is not something\n  callers should depend on here.\n- No session or no cookie → `401`, with a body that (case-insensitively)\n  contains \"oauth\" (e.g. point at `gamepigeon auth oauth google` / `github` as\n  a next step).\n- On success, append `token=<cookie token>` to `next`'s existing query\n  (preserving anything already there, e.g. `state`) and respond `200` with an\n  HTML page that redirects the browser to that URL. The response body must\n  contain the literal, un-escaped resulting URL somewhere in it — e.g. inside\n  an inline `<script>` performing the redirect (`location.replace(...)`), in\n  addition to any HTML-escaped `<meta http-equiv=\"refresh\">` copy kept for\n  browsers with scripting disabled.\n\n### `GET /cli/oauth`\n\n- Reject any `provider` other than `google`/`github` with `400`.\n- If the requested provider has no client id/secret configured in the\n  environment, respond `503`.\n- Validate `next` the same way `/cli/handoff` does, before starting sign-in.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e0870986-7b08-4e4b-992c-810d938ae282","uuid":"61df3dbe-c500-4265-8a48-b38354e827ce","timestamp":"2026-09-09T13:42:43.053Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"README.md\nassets\nbuildspec.yml\nbun.lock\ncloudflare\ncontracts\ndocs\nhooks\nhooks.json\nissue-205-fixed.png\nnode_modules\noverlay\noverlay-release.json\npackage.json\npackages\nscripts\nskills\nsrc\ntauri\ntsconfig.json\n---\n# CLI/browser OAuth handoff — API contract\n\nThis pins down the parts of the browser OAuth handoff (instruction.md) that\nare exercised by direct, white-box tests against exported functions and JSON\nerror/success bodies, rather than by end-to-end behavior alone. D1.\nOffline single-device gameplay does not require this Worker.\n\nThis Worker does not store interaction traces, completed-game results, local\nscores, or PostHog analytics. Trace and result sync belongs to\n[`adavyas/retro-backend`](https://github.com/adavyas/retro-backend); see\n[`docs/live-account-sync.md`](../../docs/live-account-sync.md) for the full\ndata ownership map.\n\n## Local setup\n\nFrom the repository root:\n\n```bash\ncd cloudflare/auth-worker\nnpm install\nnpm run cf-typegen\nnpm test\nnpm run dev\n```\n\nUse Node.js `22.6.0` or newer. The smoke command runs its TypeScript entrypoint\nwith Node's `--experimental-strip-types` support and does not add a transpiler\ndependency.\n\n`npm run dev` runs `wrangler dev --env dev`. Wrangler keeps the development D1\ndatabase and Durable Object SQLite data in its local state directory. The\n`env.dev` configuration uses:\n\n- `BETTER_AUTH_URL=http://localhost:8787`\n- `PUBLIC_GAME_URL=http://localhost:5173`\n\nCreate `cloudflare/auth-worker/.dev.vars` with development-only Better Auth and\nmigration secrets. Do not commit this file.\n\n```dotenv\nBETTER_AUTH_SECRET=replace-with-a-long-random-development-secret\nMIGRATION_SECRET=replace-with-a-different-long-random-development-secret\n```\n\nWith the local Worker running, initialize the Better Auth tables once:\n\n```bash\ncurl -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer replace-with-a-different-long-random-development-secret\" \\\n  http://localhost:8787/migrate\n```\n\nPoint a local client at the Worker with\n`GAMEPIGEON_AUTH_URL=http://localhost:8787` and\n`GAMEPIGEON_LOGIN_URL=http://localhost:5173/login.html`.\n\n## Cloudflare configuration\n\n### D1\n\nCreate the production D1 database and place its ID in the top-level\n`database_id` in `wrangler.toml`:\n\n```bash\nnpx wrangler login\nnpx wrangler d1 create tui-gamepigeon-auth\n```\n\nThe `DB` binding stores Better Auth data, initialized by calling\n`POST /migrate` with the operator bearer secret after an approved deployment.\n\n`wrangler.toml` still lists a retired `GameRoom` Durable Object migration\n(`v1` create, `v2` delete) from a multiplayer feature that has since been\nremoved. Cloudflare requires migration history to stay append-only, so those\nentries remain even though no code references `GameRoom` anymore.\n\nThe repository keeps `wrangler.toml`: Wrangler supports both TOML and JSONC,\nand changing formats would not change the deployment. The top-level\nconfiguration is production. `env.dev` is local-only and is named\n`tui-gamepigeon-auth-dev` so it cannot be confused with production. There is no\n`env.staging` until an operator provisions a separate staging Worker and D1\ndatabase; never point a future staging binding at the production database ID.\n\n### Public URLs and origins\n\nSet these non-secret variables in the relevant Wrangler environment:\n\n- `BETTER_AUTH_URL`: absolute public Worker URL, with no trailing slash.\n- `BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS`: comma-separated origins Better Auth may trust\n  for cross-origin auth requests. Keep the CLI callback origin and Worker origin.\n- `PUBLIC_GAME_URL`: absolute HTTPS URL for the app.\n\nExample production values:\n\n```toml\n[vars]\nBETTER_AUTH_URL = \"https://auth.runretroarcade.com\"\nBETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS = \"http://127.0.0.1:3210,https://auth.runretroarcade.com,https://runretroarcade.com\"\nPUBLIC_GAME_URL = \"https://runretroarcade.com\"\n```\n\n### Secrets\n\n`BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` and the separate operator-only `MIGRATION_SECRET` are\nrequired. Store both as Wrangler secrets, never in `wrangler.toml` or source\ncontrol.\n\n```bash\nopenssl rand -base64 32 | npx wrangler secret put BETTER_AUTH_SECRET\nopenssl rand -base64 32 | npx wrangler secret put MIGRATION_SECRET\n```\n\nBearer session tokens are sent as `Authorization: Bearer <token>`.\n\n## Staging gate\n\nRun all three commands before requesting a staging deployment:\n\n```bash\ncd cloudflare/auth-worker\nnpm test\nnpm run typecheck\nnpm run deploy -- --dry-run\n```\n\n`npm test` runs the Worker Vitest suite. The deploy script explicitly selects\nthe top-level production configuration with `--env=\"\"`; the extra `--dry-run`\nprevents deployment. This repository does not define `env.staging`. Before an\nactual staging deployment, an operator must define the intended staging\nenvironment with separate resources and obtain deployment approval.\n\nA production deployment requires separate explicit approval. The implementation\nand local verification documented here do not deploy this Worker.\n\n## Security notes\n\n- Keep `MIGRATION_SECRET` operator-only; `POST /migrate` rejects missing or\n  invalid {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_bdrk_01XRrzrj3YvrvDsDataU58pc","type":"tool_result","content":"// Percent-decoding for human-readable query text (OAuth error descriptions).\n// Tokens deliberately skip this: they round-trip through the listener verbatim.\nfn percent_decode(value: &str) -> String {\n    // Byte-wise on purpose: slicing `value` by byte offsets would panic on a\n    // `%` that lands mid-character in malformed input.\n    fn hex(byte: u8) -> Option<u8> {\n        match byte {\n            b'0'..=b'9' => Some(byte - b'0'),\n            b'a'..=b'f' => Some(byte - b'a' + 10),\n            b'A'..=b'F' => Some(byte - b'A' + 10),\n            _ => None,\n        }\n    }\n    let bytes = value.as_bytes();\n    let mut out: Vec<u8> = Vec::with_capacity(bytes.len());\n    let mut index = 0;\n    while index < bytes.len() {\n        match (bytes[index], bytes.get(index + 1), bytes.get(index + 2)) {\n            (b'%', Some(&high), Some(&low)) => match (hex(high), hex(low)) {\n                (Some(high), Some(low)) => {\n                    out.push(high * 16 + low);\n                    index += 3;\n                }\n                _ => {\n                    out.push(b'%');\n                    index += 1;\n                }\n            },\n            (byte, _, _) => {\n                out.push(byte);\n                index += 1;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    String::from_utf8_lossy(&out).into_owned()\n}\n\n// Turns a failed handoff (`?error=...`) into a sentence worth showing. Capped\n// because it lands in both the browser page and the overlay's status line.\nfn callback_failure(query: &str) -> Option<String> {\n    let error = query_param(query, \"error\").filter(|error| !error.is_empty())?;\n    if error == \"access_denied\" {\n        return Some(\"Sign-in was cancelled before it finished.\".to_string());\n    }\n    let message = match query_param(query, \"error_description\")\n        .map(|description| percent_decode(&description))\n        .filter(|description| !description.is_empty())\n    {\n        Some(description) => description,\n        None => format!(\n            \"The login page reported an error: {}\",\n            percent_decode(&error)\n        ),\n    };\n    Some(message.chars().take(200).collect())\n}\n\n// Handles one localhost connection using a minimal HTTP/1.1 response. Replies to\n// /auth/ping so the hosted login page's readiness probe passes, and extracts the\n// session token from /auth/callback?state=<nonce>&token=... — only when the state\n// matches the nonce this attempt issued.\nfn handle_callback_connection(\n    mut stream: std::net::TcpStream,\n    expected_state: &str,\n) -> CallbackOutcome {\n    let mut buffer = [0u8; 4096];\n    let read = match stream.read(&mut buffer) {\n        Ok(read) => read,\n        Err(_) => return CallbackOutcome::Pending,\n    };\n    let request = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buffer[..read]);\n    let target = request\n        .lines()\n        .next()\n        .and_then(|line| line.split_whitespace().nth(1))\n        .unwrap_or(\"/\");\n    let (path, query) = match target.split_once('?') {\n        Some((path, query)) => (path, query),\n        None => (target, \"\"),\n    };\n\n    let mut reply = |status: &str, content_type: &str, body: &str| {\n        let response = format!(\n            // Pages carry a session token in their URL — keep them out of\n            // caches and out of the Referer sent to anything they link to.\n            \"HTTP/1.1 {status}\\r\\nContent-Type: {content_type}\\r\\nContent-Length: {}\\r\\nCache-Control: no-store\\r\\nReferrer-Policy: no-referrer\\r\\nConnection: close\\r\\n\\r\\n{body}\",\n            body.len(),\n        );\n        let _ = stream.write_all(response.as_bytes());\n        let _ = stream.flush();\n    };\n\n    if path == \"/auth/ping\" {\n        reply(\"200 OK\", \"text/plain; charset=utf-8\", \"ok\");\n        return CallbackOutcome::Pending;\n    }\n    if path != \"/auth/callback\" {\n        reply(\"404 Not Found\", \"text/plain; charset=utf-8\", \"not found\");\n        return CallbackOutcome::Pending;\n    }\n    // Reject any callback whose state doesn't match this attempt's nonce, so a\n    // racing local client cannot substitute its own account via our listener.\n    // A stale tab from a cancelled attempt lands here too, so the page explains\n    // itself rather than showing a bare \"forbidden\".\n    let state = query_param(query, \"state\").unwrap_or_default();\n    if state != expected_state {\n        reply(\n            \"403 Forbidden\",\n            \"text/html; b'0'),\n            b'a'..=b'f' => Some(byte - b'a' + 10),\n            b'A'..=b'F' => Some(byte - b'A' + 10),\n            _ => None,\n        }\n    }\n    let bytes = value.as_bytes();\n    let mut out: Vec<u8> = Vec::with_capacity(bytes.len());\n    let mut index = 0;\n    while index < bytes.len() {\n        match (bytes[index], bytes.get(index + 1), bytes.get(index + 2)) {\n            (b'%', Some(&high), Some(&low)) => match (hex(high), hex(low)) {\n                (Some(high), Some(low)) => {\n                    out.push(high * 16 + low);\n                    index += 3;\n                }\n                _ => {\n                    out.push(b'%');\n                    index += 1;\n                }\n            },\n            (byte, _, _) => {\n                out.push(byte);\n                index += 1;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    String::from_utf8_lossy(&out).into_owned()\n}\n\n// Turns a failed handoff (`?error=...`) into a sentence worth showing. aria-hidden=\"true\"><circle cx=\"12\" cy=\"12\" r=\"9\"/><path d=\"M12 7.5v5.5\"/><path d=\"M12 16.5h.01\"/></svg>\"#;\n\npub fn escape_html(value: &str) -> String {\n    let mut out = String::with_capacity(value.len());\n    for character in value.chars() {\n        match character {\n            '&' => out.push_str(\"&amp;\"),\n            '<' => out.push_str(\"&lt;\"),\n            '>' => out.push_str(\"&gt;\"),\n            '\"' => out.push_str(\"&quot;\"),\n            '\\'' => out.push_str(\"&#39;\"),\n            _ => out.push(character),\n        }\n    }\n    out\n}\n\n/// Renders one self-contained Tui page — no external CSS, fonts, or images.\n///\n/// `lead`, `steps`, and `hint` are trusted HTML so copy can carry `<code>`\n/// spans; the title is escaped. Escape request-derived text at the call site.\nfn arcade_page(tone: &str, title: &str, lead: &str, steps: &[&str], hint: &str) -> String {\n    let list = if steps.is_empty() {\n        String::new()\n    } else {\n        format!(\n            \"<ul class=\\\"steps\\\">{}</ul>\",\n            steps\n                .iter()\n                .map(|step| format!(\"<li>{step}</li>\"))\n                .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n                .join(\"\"),\n        )\n    };\n    let hint = if hint.is_empty() {\n        String::new()\n    } else {\n        format!(\"<p class=\\\"hint\\\">{hint}</p>\")\n    };\n    let icon = if tone == \"ok\" { CHECK_ICON } else { ALERT_ICON };\n    format!(\n        r#\"<!doctype html>\n<html lang=\"en\">\n<head>\n<meta charset=\"utf-8\">\n<meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1\">\n<meta name=\"robots\" content=\"noindex\">\n<title>Tui — {title_text}</title>\n<style>{STYLE}</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n<main class=\"card\" data-tone=\"{tone}\">\n<span class=\"badge\">{icon}</span>\n<p class=\"eyebrow\">Tui</p>\n<h1>{title_text}</h1>\n<p class=\"lead\">{lead}</p>\n{list}\n{hint}\n</main>\n</body>\n</html>\"#,\n        title_text = escape_html(title),\n    )\n}\n\n/// Success: the overlay has the token and is leaving the sign-in gate.\npub fn signed_in_page() -> String {\n    arcade_page(\n        \"ok\",\n        \"You're signed in\",\n        \"Tui is unlocking now. You can close this tab and go back to the overlay.\",\n        &[],\n        \"Your session is saved on this machine, so Tui opens straight to the games next time.\",\n    )\n}\n\n/// The handoff reached us but carried no usable session.\npub fn sign_in_failed_page(reason: &str) -> String {\n    arcade_page(\n        \"warn\",\n        \"Sign-in didn't finish\",\n        reason,\n        &[\n            \"Go back to Tui and choose <b>Sign in</b> again.\",\n            \"If your browser blocked the redirect back to Tui, allow pop-ups for the login page.\",\n            \"Signing in with Google or GitHub? Go back to Tui and choose <b>Sign in</b> again.\",\n        ],\n        \"\",\n    )\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn escapes_html_in_page_titles_and_text() {\n        assert_eq!(escape_html(\"a<b>&\\\"'\"), \"a&lt;b&gt;&amp;&quot;&#39;\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn success_page_is_a_branded_standalone_document() {\n        let page = signed_in_page();\n        assert!(page.starts_with(\"<!doctype html>\"));\n        assert!(page.contains(\"You&#39;re signed in\"));\n        assert!(page.contains(\"close this tab\"));\n        assert!(page.contains(\"Tui\"));\n        // Self-contained: nothing to fetch from the network.\n        assert!(!page.contains(\"http://\"));\n        assert!(!page.contains(\"https://\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn failure_pages_say_what_to_do_next() {\n        let failed = sign_in_failed_page(\"The login page came back without a session token.\");\n        assert!(failed.contains(\"without a session token\"));\n        assert!(failed.contains(\"Sign in</b> again\"));\n\n        let stale = stale_attempt_page();\n        assert!(stale.contains(\"out of date\"));\n        assert!(stale.contains(\"Sign in</b> again\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn failure_reason_is_escaped_by_the_caller_not_injected_raw() {\n        // Callers escape request-derived text; the page must carry it verbatim\n        // so already-escaped entities are not double-escaped.\n        let page = sign_in_failed_page(&escape_html(\"<script>alert(1)</script>\"));\n        assert!(!page.contains(\"<script>\"));\n        assert!(page.contains(\"&lt;script&gt;\"));\n    }\n}","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false}}

Mirrors the overlay's Rust flow in\n// `tauri/src/main.rs`; docs/oauth-handoff-contract.md pins the shapes and trust\n// rules both sides rely on.\n\nexport interface OAuthCallbackServer {\n  /** The port actually bound, which may differ from the requested one. */\n  readonly port: number;\n  /** Resolves once the socket is bound and `port` has its final value. */\n  ready: Promise<void>;\n  /** Resolves with the raw token from the callback, or rejects on error/cancel. */\n  token: Promise<string>;\n  close(): void;\n}\n\n/**\n * Reads one query parameter verbatim, without decoding. Tolerant of both `&`\n * and `?` separators: some hosted redirects append `?token` onto a callback\n * that already carried `?state`, yielding `state=...?token=...`.\n */\nfunction queryParam(query: string, key: string): string | undefined {\n  for (const pair of query.split(/[&?]/)) {\n    const separator = pair.indexOf(\"=\");\n    if (separator !== -1 && pair.slice(0, separator) === key) return pair.slice(separator + 1);\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\n/**\n * Percent-decoding for human-readable query text (OAuth error descriptions).\n * Tokens deliberately skip this: they round-trip through the listener verbatim.\n */\nfunction decodeQueryText(value: string): string {\n  const spaced = value.replace(/\\+/g, \" \");\n  try {\n    return decodeURIComponent(spaced);\n  } catch {\n    return spaced;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Turns a failed handoff (`?error=...`) into a sentence worth showing. Capped\n * because it lands in both the browser page and the terminal.\n */\nfunction callbackFailure(query: string): string | undefined {\n  const error = queryParam(query, \"error\");\n  if (!error) return undefined;\n  if (error === \"access_denied\") return \"Sign-in was cancelled before it finished.\";\n  const described = decodeQueryText(queryParam(query, \"error_description\") ?? \"\");\n  const message = described || `The login page reported an error: ${decodeQueryText(error)}`;\n  return [...message].slice(0, 200).join(\"\");\n}\n\nfunction handleCallbackRequest(\n  request: IncomingMessage,\n  response: ServerResponse,\n  resolveToken: (token: string) => void,\n  rejectToken: (error: Error) => void,\n): void {\n  const target = request.url ?? \"/\";\n  const separator = target.indexOf(\"?\");\n  const path = separator === -1 ? target : target.slice(0, separator);\n  const query = separator === -1 ? \"\" : target.slice(separator + 1);\n\n  const reply = (status: number, contentType: string, body: string): void => {\n    response.writeHead(status, {\n      \"content-type\": contentType,\n      \"content-length\": Buffer.byteLength(body),\n      // Pages carry a session token in their URL — keep them out of caches and\n      // out of the Referer sent to anything they link to.\n      \"cache-control\": \"no-store\",\n      \"referrer-policy\": \"no-referrer\",\n      connection: \"close\",\n    });\n    response.end(body);\n  };\n\n  // The Worker probes this before redirecting, so a stale tab pointed at a dead\n  // port fails fast instead of handing a token to nothing.\n  if (path === \"/auth/ping\") {\n    reply(200, \"text/plain; charset=utf-8\", \"ok\");\n    return;\n  }\n  if (path !== \"/auth/callback\") {\n    reply(404, \"text/plain; charset=utf-8\", \"not found\");\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // A login page that gave up hands back `error` instead of `token`; saying why\n  // beats the generic \"did not return a session token\".\n  const failure = callbackFailure(query);\n  if (failure) {\n    reply(400, \"text/html; charset=utf-8\", signInFailedPage(escapeHtml(failure)));\n    rejectToken(new Error(failure));\n    return;\n  }\n  const token = queryParam(query, \"token\");\n  if (!token) {\n    const reason = \"The login page came back without a session token.\";\n    reply(400, \"text/html; charset=utf-8\", signInFailedPage(escapeHtml(reason)));\n    rejectToken(new Error(reason));\n    return;\n  }\n  reply(200, \"text/html; charset=utf-8\", signedInPage());\n  resolveToken(token);\n}\n\n/**\n * Starts the localhost listener the browser hands the finished session back to.\n *\n * Returns synchronously so the caller can build the callback URL from `port`\n * after awaiting `ready`. The token arrives raw and unverified: unlike the\n * overlay's Rust listener, which generates its own `state` nonce and can\n * therefore reject a mismatch, this takes only a port and has nothing to\n * compare against — any `state` is accepted and ignored here, and verifying the\n * token against the auth backend is the caller's job.\n */\nexport function startOAuthCallbackServer(port: number = defaultCallbackPort): OAuthCallbackServer {\n  let boundPort = port;\n  let resolveToken!: (token: string) => void;\n  let rejectToken!: (error: Error) => void;\n  const token = new Promise<string>((resolve, reject) => {\n    resolveToken = resolve;\n    rejectToken = reject;\n  });\n  // A caller that closes the listener without awaiting `token` (because it gave\n  // up first) must not turn a late rejection into an unhandled-rejection crash.\n  // The promise still rejects for anyone who does await it.\n  token.catch(() => {});\n\n  const server = createServer((request, response) => {\n    handleCallbackRequest(request, response, resolveToken, rejectToken);\n  });\n\n  const ready = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {\n    let retried = false;\n    server.on(\"listening\", () => {\n      const address = server.address();\n      if (address && typeof address === \"object\") boundPort = address.port;\n      resolve();\n    });\n    server.on(\"error\", (error: NodeJS.ErrnoException) => {\n      // Someone else already owns the preferred port — usually the overlay's\n      // listener or another sign-in in flight. One retry on an OS-assigned port\n      // keeps the handoff working instead of failing outright.\n      if (!retried && error.code === \"EADDRINUSE\") {\n        retried = true;\n        server.listen(0, \"127.0.0.1\");\n        return;\n      }\n      reject(error);\n    });\n  });\n  server.listen(port, \"127.0.0.1\");\n\n  return {\n    get port() {\n      return boundPort;\n    },\n    ready,\n    token,\n    close() {\n      server.closeAllConnections?.();\n      server.close();\n    },\n  };\n}\n\n/** Runs an opener and reports whether it actually succeeded — `xdg-open` with\n * no handler and `open` with a broken default browser both fail *after* the\n * spawn, so the exit status is the only honest signal. Every opener returns\n * promptly once the URL is handed to the browser. */\nfunction runOpener(command: string, args: string[]): boolean {\n  try {\n    return spawnSync(command, args, { stdio: \"ignore\" }).status === 0;\n  } catch {\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n\nfunction applescriptEscape(value: string): string {\n  return value.replace(/\\\\/g, \"\\\\\\\\\").replace(/\"/g, '\\\\\"');\n}\n\n/**\n * Arc (and Dia, same vendor) route links from other apps into a \"Little Arc\"\n * window, which drops the Worker's redirect chain — the tab either never\n * appears or dies mid-handoff, so sign-in silently never completes.\n */\nexport function isArcBundle(bundleId: string): boolean {\n  const id = bundleId.toLowerCase();\n  return id === \"company.thebrowser.browser\" || id === \"company.thebrowser.dia\";\n}\n\n/** Asking Arc for a real tab in a real window over AppleScript bypasses Little\n * Arc entirely. Kept identical to the overlay's `open_in_arc` script. */\nexport function arcTabScript(bundleId: string, url: string): string {\n  return `tell application id \"${applescriptEscape(bundleId)}\"\n    activate\n    if (count of windows) is 0 then\n        make new window\n    end if\n    tell front window to make new tab with properties {URL:\"${applescriptEscape(url)}\"}\nend tell`;\n}\n\n/**\n * `defaults read` prints one dict per registered handler; the role and the\n * scheme are separate lines inside the same `{ ... }` block, so we scan block by\n * block for the one claiming `https`.\n */\nfunction parseDefaultHttpsHandler(plist: string): string | undefined {\n  for (const block of plist.split(\"{\")) {\n    if (!block.includes(\"LSHandlerURLScheme = https;\")) continue;\n    const line = block.split(\"\\n\").find((entry) => entry.trimStart().startsWith(\"LSHandlerRoleAll = \"));\n    const value = line\n      ?.trim()\n      .replace(/^LSHandlerRoleAll = /, \"\")\n      .replace(/;$/, \"\")\n      .replace(/^\"|\"$/g, \"\");\n    if (value) return value;\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\n/** The bundle id macOS would hand an `https` URL to, read from LaunchServices.\n * Used only to detect Arc; any parse failure just means the ordinary `open`\n * path is taken. */\nfunction defaultHttpsHandler(): string | undefined {\n  try {\n    const result = spawnSync(\n      \"defaults\",\n      [\"read\", \"com.apple.LaunchServices/com.apple.launchservices.secure\"],\n      { encoding: \"utf8\" },\n    );\n    if (result.status !== 0 || !result.stdout) return undefined;\n    return parseDefaultHttpsHandler(result.stdout);\n  } catch {\n    return undefined;\n  }\n}\n\n/** Hands the sign-in URL to the user's browser. Returns false when every opener\n * we know about failed, so the caller can lead with the copyable link instead of\n * claiming a browser is opening. */\nfunction openBrowser(url: string): boolean {\n  if (process.platform === \"darwin\") {\n    const bundleId = defaultHttpsHandler();\n    if (bundleId && isArcBundle(bundleId) && runOpener(\"osascript\", [\"-e\", arcTabScript(bundleId, url)])) {\n      return true;\n    }\n    // Safari is the last resort: it is always present, so a broken or missing\n    // default handler still gets the user to the login page.\n    return runOpener(\"open\", [url]) || runOpener(\"open\", [\"-a\", \"Safari\", url]);\n  }\n  if (process.platform === \"win32\") {\n    return runOpener(\"cmd\", [\"/c\", \"start\", \"\", url])\n      || runOpener(\"rundll32\", [\"url.dll,FileProtocolHandler\", url]);\n  }\n  return runOpener(\"xdg-open\", [url])\n    || runOpener(\"gio\", [\"open\", url])\n    || runOpener(\"x-www-browser\", [url]);\n}\n\nasync function runOAuthLogin(provider: OAuthProvider): Promise<AuthSuccess> {\n  const authBaseUrl = requireAuthBaseUrl();\n  const callback = startOAuthCallbackServer(defaultCallbackPort);\n  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;\n  try {\n    await callback.ready;\n    const next = `http://127.0.0.1:${callback.port}/auth/callback`;\n    const signInUrl = `${authBaseUrl}/cli/oauth?provider=${provider}&next=${\n      encodeURIComponent(next)\n    }`;\n    if (openBrowser(signInUrl)) {\n      console.log(`Opening your browser to finish signing in with ${provider}.`);\n      console.log(`If nothing opened, visit:\\n  ${signInUrl}`);\n    } else {\n      console.log(`Could not open a browser. Mirrors the overlay's Rust flow in\n// `tauri/src/main.rs`; docs/oauth-handoff-contract.md pins the shapes and trust\n// rules both sides rely on.\n\nexport interface OAuthCallbackServer {\n  /** The port actually bound, which may differ from the requested one. */\n  readonly port: number;\n  /** Resolves once the socket is bound and `port` has its final value. */\n  ready: Promise<void>;\n  /** Resolves with the raw token from the callback, or rejects on error/cancel. */\n  token: Promise<string>;\n  close(): void;\n}\n\n/**\n * Reads one query parameter verbatim, without decoding. Tolerant of both `&`\n * and `?` separators: some hosted redirects append `?token` onto a callback\n * that already carried `?state`, yielding `state=...?token=...`.\n */\nfunction queryParam(query: string, key: string): string | undefined {\n  for (const pair of query.split(/[&?]/)) {\n    const separator = pair.indexOf(\"=\");\n    if (separator !== -1 && pair.slice(0, separator) === key) return pair.slice(separator + 1);\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\n/**\n * Percent-decoding for human-readable query text (OAuth error descriptions).\n * Tokens deliberately skip this: they round-trip through the listener verbatim.\n */\nfunction decodeQueryText(value: string): string {\n  const spaced = value.replace(/\\+/g, \" \");\n  try {\n    return decodeURIComponent(spaced);\n  } catch {\n    return spaced;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Turns a failed handoff (`?error=...`) into a sentence worth showing. Capped\n * because it lands in both the browser page and the terminal.\n */\nfunction callbackFailure(query: string): string | undefined {\n  const error = queryParam(query, \"error\");\n  if (!error) return undefined;\n  if (error === \"access_denied\") return \"Sign-in was cancelled before it finished.\";\n  const described = decodeQueryText(queryParam(query, \"error_description\") ?? \"\");\n  const message = described || `The login page reported an error: ${decodeQueryText(error)}`;\n  return [...message].slice(0, 200).join(\"\");\n}\n\nfunction handleCallbackRequest(\n  request: IncomingMessage,\n  response: ServerResponse,\n  resolveToken: (token: string) => void,\n  rejectToken: (error: Error) => void,\n): void {\n  const target = request.url ?? Tolerant of both `&`\n * and `?` separators: some hosted redirects append `?token` onto a callback\n * that already carried `?state`, yielding `state=...?token=...`.\n */\nfunction queryParam(query: string, key: string): string | undefined {\n  for (const pair of query.split(/[&?]/)) {\n    const separator = pair.indexOf(\"=\");\n    if (separator !== -1 && pair.slice(0, separator) === key) return pair.slice(separator + 1);\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\n/**\n * Percent-decoding for human-readable query text (OAuth error descriptions).\n * Tokens deliberately skip this: they round-trip through the listener verbatim.\n */\nfunction decodeQueryText(value: string): string {\n  const spaced = value.replace(/\\+/g, \" \");\n  try {\n    return decodeURIComponent(spaced);\n  } catch {\n    return spaced;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Turns a failed handoff (`?error=...`) into a sentence worth showing. \"\");\n  const message = described || `The login page reported an error: ${decodeQueryText(error)}`;\n  return [...message].slice(0, 200).join(\"\");\n}\n\nfunction handleCallbackRequest(\n  request: IncomingMessage,\n  response: ServerResponse,\n  resolveToken: (token: string) => void,\n  rejectToken: (error: Error) => void,\n): void {\n  const target = request.url ?? \"/\";\n  const separator = target.indexOf(\"?\");\n  const path = separator === -1 ? target : target.slice(0, separator);\n  const query = separator === -1 ? \"\" : target.slice(separator + 1);\n\n  const reply = (status: number, contentType: string, body: string): void => {\n    response.writeHead(status, {\n      \"content-type\": contentType,\n      \"content-length\": Buffer.byteLength(body),\n      // Pages carry a session token in their URL — keep them out of caches and\n      // out of the Referer sent to anything they link to.\n      \"cache-control\": \"no-store\",\n      \"referrer-policy\": \"no-referrer\",\n      connection: \"close\",\n    });\n    response.end(body);\n  };\n\n  // The Worker probes this before redirecting, so a stale tab pointed at a dead\n  // port fails fast instead of handing a token to nothing.\n  if (path === \"/auth/ping\") {\n    reply(200, \"text/plain; charset=utf-8\", \"ok\");\n    return;\n  }\n  if (path !== \"/auth/callback\") {\n    reply(404, \"text/plain; charset=utf-8\", \"not found\");\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // A login page that gave up hands back `error` instead of `token`; saying why\n  // beats the generic \"did not return a session token\".\n  const failure = callbackFailure(query);\n  if (failure) {\n    reply(400, \"text/html; charset=utf-8\", signInFailedPage(escapeHtml(failure)));\n    rejectToken(new Error(failure));\n    return;\n  }\n  const token = queryParam(query, \"token\");\n  if (!token) {\n    const reason = \"The login page came back without a session token.\";\n    reply(400, \"text/html; charset=utf-8\", signInFailedPage(escapeHtml(reason)));\n    rejectToken(new Error(reason));\n    return;\n  }\n  reply(200, \"text/html; charset=utf-8\", signedInPage());\n  resolveToken(token);\n}\n\n/**\n * Starts the localhost listener the browser hands the finished session back to.\n *\n * Returns synchronously so the caller can build the callback URL from `port`\n * after awaiting `ready`. The token arrives raw and unverified: unlike the\n * overlay's Rust listener, which generates its own `state` nonce and can\n * therefore reject a mismatch, this takes only a port and has nothing to\n * compare against — any `state` is accepted and ignored here, and verifying the\n * token against the auth backend is the caller's job.\n */\nexport function startOAuthCallbackServer(port: number = defaultCallbackPort): OAuthCallbackServer {\n  let boundPort = port;\n  let resolveToken!: (token: string) => void;\n  let rejectToken!: (error: Error) => void;\n  const token = new Promise<string>((resolve, reject) => {\n    resolveToken = resolve;\n    rejectToken = reject;\n  });\n  // A caller that closes the listener without awaiting `token` (because it gave\n  // up first) must not turn a late rejection into an unhandled-rejection crash.\n  // The promise still rejects for anyone who does await it.\n  token.catch(() => {});\n\n  const server = createServer((request, response) => {\n    handleCallbackRequest(request, response, resolveToken, rejectToken);\n  });\n\n  const ready = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {\n    let retried = false;\n    server.on(\"listening\", () => {\n      const address = server.address();\n      if (address && typeof address === \"object\") boundPort = address.port;\n      resolve();\n    });\n    server.on(\"error\", (error: NodeJS.ErrnoException) => {\n      // Someone else already owns the preferred port — usually the overlay's\n      // listener or another sign-in in flight. One retry on an OS-assigned port\n      // keeps the handoff working instead of failing outright.\n      if (!retried && error.code === \"EADDRINUSE\") {\n        retried = true;\n        server.listen(0, \"127.0.0.1\");\n        return;\n      }\n      reject(error);\n    });\n  });\n  server.listen(port, \"127.0.0.1\");\n\n  return {\n    get port() {\n      return boundPort;\n    },\n    ready,\n    token,\n    close() {\n      server.closeAllConnections?.();\n      server.close();\n    },\n  };\n}\n\n/** Runs an opener and reports whether it actually succeeded — `xdg-open` with\n * no handler and `open` with a broken default browser both fail *after* the\n * spawn, so the exit status is the only honest signal. Every opener returns\n * promptly once the URL is handed to the browser. */\nfunction runOpener(command: string, args: string[]): boolean {\n  try {\n    return spawnSync(command, args, { stdio: \"ignore\" }).status === 0;\n  } catch {\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n\nfunction applescriptEscape(value: string): string {\n  return value.replace(/\\\\/g, \"\\\\\\\\\").replace(/\"/g, '\\\\\"');\n}\n\n/**\n * Arc (and Dia, same vendor) route links from other apps into a \"Little Arc\"\n * window, which drops the Worker's redirect chain — the tab either never\n * appears or dies mid-handoff, so sign-in silently never completes.\n */\nexport function isArcBundle(bundleId: string): boolean {\n  const id = bundleId.toLowerCase();\n  return id === \"company.thebrowser.browser\" || id === \"company.thebrowser.dia\";\n}\n\n/** Asking Arc for a real tab in a real window over AppleScript bypasses Little\n * Arc entirely. Kept identical to the overlay's `open_in_arc` script. */\nexport function arcTabScript(bundleId: string, url: string): string {\n  return `tell application id \"${applescriptEscape(bundleId)}\"\n    activate\n    if (count of windows) is 0 then\n        make new window\n    end if\n    tell front window to make new tab with properties {URL:\"${applescriptEscape(url)}\"}\nend tell`;\n}\n\n/**\n * `defaults read` prints one dict per registered handler; the role and the\n * scheme are separate lines inside the same `{ ... }` block, so we scan block by\n * block for the one claiming `https`.\n */\nfunction parseDefaultHttpsHandler(plist: string): string | undefined {\n  for (const block of plist.split(\"{\")) {\n    if (!block.includes(\"LSHandlerURLScheme = https;\")) continue;\n    const line = block.split(\"\\n\").find((entry) => entry.trimStart().startsWith(\"LSHandlerRoleAll = \"));\n    const value = line\n      ?.trim()\n      .replace(/^LSHandlerRoleAll = /, \"\")\n      .replace(/;$/, \"\")\n      .replace(/^\"|\"$/g, \"\");\n    if (value) return value;\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\n/** The bundle id macOS would hand an `https` URL to, read from LaunchServices.\n * Used only to detect Arc; any parse failure just means the ordinary `open`\n * path is taken. */\nfunction defaultHttpsHandler(): string | undefined {\n  try {\n    const result = spawnSync(\n      \"defaults\",\n      [\"read\", \"com.apple.LaunchServices/com.apple.launchservices.secure\"],\n      { encoding: \"utf8\" },\n    );\n    if (result.status !== 0 || !result.stdout) return undefined;\n    return parseDefaultHttpsHandler(result.stdout);\n  } catch {\n    return undefined;\n  }\n}\n\n/** Hands the sign-in URL to the user's browser. Returns false when every opener\n * we know about failed, so the caller can lead with the copyable link instead of\n * claiming a browser is opening. */\nfunction openBrowser(url: string): boolean {\n  if (process.platform === \"darwin\") {\n    const bundleId = defaultHttpsHandler();\n    if (bundleId && isArcBundle(bundleId) && runOpener(\"osascript\", [\"-e\", arcTabScript(bundleId, url)])) {\n      return true;\n    }\n    // Safari is the last resort: it is always present, so a broken or missing\n    // default handler still gets the user to the login page.\n    return runOpener(\"open\", [url]) || runOpener(\"open\", [\"-a\", \"Safari\", url]);\n  }\n  if (process.platform === \"win32\") {\n    return runOpener(\"cmd\", [\"/c\", \"start\", \"\", url])\n      || runOpener(\"rundll32\", [\"url.dll,FileProtocolHandler\", url]);\n  }\n  return runOpener(\"xdg-open\", [url])\n    || runOpener(\"gio\", [\"open\", url])\n    || runOpener(\"x-www-browser\", [url]);\n}\n\nasync function runOAuthLogin(provider: OAuthProvider): Promise<AuthSuccess> {\n  const authBaseUrl = requireAuthBaseUrl();\n  const callback = startOAuthCallbackServer(defaultCallbackPort);\n  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;\n  try {\n    await callback.ready;\n    const next = `http://127.0.0.1:${callback.port}/auth/callback`;\n    const signInUrl = `${authBaseUrl}/cli/oauth?provider=${provider}&next=${\n      encodeURIComponent(next)\n    }`;\n    if (openBrowser(signInUrl)) {\n      console.log(`Opening your browser to finish signing in with ${provider}.`);\n      console.log(`If nothing opened, visit:\\n  ${signInUrl}`);\n    } else {\n      console.log(`Could not open a browser. Capped\n * because it lands in both the browser page and the terminal.\n */\nfunction callbackFailure(query: string): string | undefined {\n  const error = queryParam(query, \"error\");\n  if (!error) return undefined;\n  if (error === \"access_denied\") return \"Sign-in was cancelled before it finished.\";\n  const described = decodeQueryText(queryParam(query, \"error_description\") ?? \"\");\n  const message = described || `The login page reported an error: ${decodeQueryText(error)}`;\n  return [...message].slice(0, 200).join(\"\");\n}\n\nfunction handleCallbackRequest(\n  request: IncomingMessage,\n  response: ServerResponse,\n  resolveToken: (token: string) => void,\n  rejectToken: (error: Error) => void,\n): void {\n  const target = request.url ?? \"/\";\n  const separator = target.indexOf(\"?\");\n  const path = separator === -1 ? target : target.slice(0, separator);\n  const query = separator === -1 ? \"\" : target.slice(separator + 1);\n\n  const reply = (status: number, contentType: string, body: string): void => {\n    response.writeHead(status, {\n      \"content-type\": contentType,\n      \"content-length\": Buffer.byteLength(body),\n      // Pages carry a session token in their URL — keep them out of caches and\n      // out of the Referer sent to anything they link to.\n      \"cache-control\": \"no-store\",\n      \"referrer-policy\": \"no-referrer\",\n      connection: \"close\",\n    });\n    response.end(body);\n  };\n\n  // The Worker probes this before redirecting, so a stale tab pointed at a dead\n  // port fails fast instead of handing a token to nothing.\n  if (path === \"/auth/ping\") {\n    reply(200, \"text/plain; charset=utf-8\", \"ok\");\n    return;\n  }\n  if (path !== \"/auth/callback\") {\n    reply(404, \"text/plain; charset=utf-8\", \"not found\");\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // A login page that gave up hands back `error` instead of `token`; saying why\n  // beats the generic \"did not return a session token\".\n  const failure = callbackFailure(query);\n  if (failure) {\n    reply(400, \"text/html; charset=utf-8\", signInFailedPage(escapeHtml(failure)));\n    rejectToken(new Error(failure));\n    return;\n  }\n  const token = queryParam(query, \"token\");\n  if (!token) {\n    const reason = \"The login page came back without a session token.\";\n    reply(400, \"text/html; charset=utf-8\", signInFailedPage(escapeHtml(reason)));\n    rejectToken(new Error(reason));\n    return;\n  }\n  reply(200, \"text/html; charset=utf-8\", signedInPage());\n  resolveToken(token);\n}\n\n/**\n * Starts the localhost listener the browser hands the finished session back to.\n *\n * Returns synchronously so the caller can build the callback URL from `port`\n * after awaiting `ready`. The token arrives raw and unverified: unlike the\n * overlay's Rust listener, which generates its own `state` nonce and can\n * therefore reject a mismatch, this takes only a port and has nothing to\n * compare against — any `state` is accepted and ignored here, and verifying the\n * token against the auth backend is the caller's job.\n */\nexport function startOAuthCallbackServer(port: number = defaultCallbackPort): OAuthCallbackServer {\n  let boundPort = port;\n  let resolveToken!: (token: string) => void;\n  let rejectToken!: (error: Error) => void;\n  const token = new Promise<string>((resolve, reject) => {\n    resolveToken = resolve;\n    rejectToken = reject;\n  });\n  // A caller that closes the listener without awaiting `token` (because it gave\n  // up first) must not turn a late rejection into an unhandled-rejection crash.\n  // The promise still rejects for anyone who does await it.\n  token.catch(() => {});\n\n  const server = createServer((request, response) => {\n    handleCallbackRequest(request, response, resolveToken, rejectToken);\n  });\n\n  const ready = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {\n    let retried = false;\n    server.on(\"listening\", () => {\n      const address = server.address();\n      if (address && typeof address === \"object\") boundPort = address.port;\n      resolve();\n    });\n    server.on(\"error\", (error: NodeJS.ErrnoException) => {\n      // Someone else already owns the preferred port — usually the overlay's\n      // listener or another sign-in in flight. One retry on an OS-assigned port\n      // keeps the handoff working instead of failing outright.\n      if (!retried && error.code === \"EADDRINUSE\") {\n        retried = true;\n        server.listen(0, \"127.0.0.1\");\n        return;\n      }\n      reject(error);\n    });\n  });\n  server.listen(port, \"127.0.0.1\");\n\n  return {\n    get port() {\n      return boundPort;\n    },\n    ready,\n    token,\n    close() {\n      server.closeAllConnections?.();\n      server.close();\n    },\n  };\n}\n\n/** Runs an opener and reports whether it actually succeeded — `xdg-open` with\n * no handler and `open` with a broken default browser both fail *after* the\n * spawn, so the exit status is the only honest signal. Every opener returns\n * promptly once the URL is handed to the browser. */\nfunction runOpener(command: string, args: string[]): boolean {\n  try {\n    return spawnSync(command, args, { stdio: \"ignore\" }).status === 0;\n  } catch {\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n\nfunction applescriptEscape(value: string): string {\n  return value.replace(/\\\\/g, \"\\\\\\\\\").replace(/\"/g, '\\\\\"');\n}\n\n/**\n * Arc (and Dia, same vendor) route links from other apps into a \"Little Arc\"\n * window, which drops the Worker's redirect chain — the tab either never\n * appears or dies mid-handoff, so sign-in silently never completes.\n */\nexport function isArcBundle(bundleId: string): boolean {\n  const id = bundleId.toLowerCase();\n  return id === \"company.thebrowser.browser\" || id === \"company.thebrowser.dia\";\n}\n\n/** Asking Arc for a real tab in a real window over AppleScript bypasses Little\n * Arc entirely. Kept identical to the overlay's `open_in_arc` script. */\nexport function arcTabScript(bundleId: string, url: string): string {\n  return `tell application id \"${applescriptEscape(bundleId)}\"\n    activate\n    if (count of windows) is 0 then\n        make new window\n    end if\n    tell front window to make new tab with properties {URL:\"${applescriptEscape(url)}\"}\nend tell`;\n}\n\n/**\n * `defaults read` prints one dict per registered handler; the role and the\n * scheme are separate lines inside the same `{ ... }` block, so we scan block by\n * block for the one claiming `https`.\n */\nfunction parseDefaultHttpsHandler(plist: string): string | undefined {\n  for (const block of plist.split(\"{\")) {\n    if (!block.includes(\"LSHandlerURLScheme = https;\")) continue;\n    const line = block.split(\"\\n\").find((entry) => entry.trimStart().startsWith(\"LSHandlerRoleAll = \"));\n    const value = line\n      ?.trim()\n      .replace(/^LSHandlerRoleAll = /, \"\")\n      .replace(/;$/, \"\")\n      .replace(/^\"|\"$/g, \"\");\n    if (value) return value;\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\n/** The bundle id macOS would hand an `https` URL to, read from LaunchServices.\n * Used only to detect Arc; any parse failure just means the ordinary `open`\n * path is taken. */\nfunction defaultHttpsHandler(): string | undefined {\n  try {\n    const result = spawnSync(\n      \"defaults\",\n      [\"read\", \"com.apple.LaunchServices/com.apple.launchservices.secure\"],\n      { encoding: \"utf8\" },\n    );\n    if (result.status !== 0 || !result.stdout) return undefined;\n    return parseDefaultHttpsHandler(result.stdout);\n  } catch {\n    return undefined;\n  }\n}\n\n/** Hands the sign-in URL to the user's browser. Tolerant of both `&`\n * and `?` separators: some hosted redirects append `?token` onto a callback\n * that already carried `?state`, yielding `state=...?token=...`.\n */\nfunction queryParam(query: string, key: string): string | undefined {\n  for (const pair of query.split(/[&?]/)) {\n    const separator = pair.indexOf(\"=\");\n    if (separator !== -1 && pair.slice(0, separator) === key) return pair.slice(separator + 1);\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\n/**\n * Percent-decoding for human-readable query text (OAuth error descriptions).\n * Tokens deliberately skip this: they round-trip through the listener verbatim.\n */\nfunction decodeQueryText(value: string): string {\n  const spaced = value.replace(/\\+/g, \" \");\n  try {\n    return decodeURIComponent(spaced);\n  } catch {\n    return spaced;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Turns a failed handoff (`?error=...`) into a sentence worth showing. \"\");\n  const message = described || `The login page reported an error: ${decodeQueryText(error)}`;\n  return [...message].slice(0, 200).join(\"\");\n}\n\nfunction handleCallbackRequest(\n  request: IncomingMessage,\n  response: ServerResponse,\n  resolveToken: (token: string) => void,\n  rejectToken: (error: Error) => void,\n): void {\n  const target = request.url ?? \"/\";\n  const separator = target.indexOf(\"?\");\n  const path = separator === -1 ? target : target.slice(0, separator);\n  const query = separator === -1 ? \"\" : target.slice(separator + 1);\n\n  const reply = (status: number, contentType: string, body: string): void => {\n    response.writeHead(status, {\n      \"content-type\": contentType,\n      \"content-length\": Buffer.byteLength(body),\n      // Pages carry a session token in their URL — keep them out of caches and\n      // out of the Referer sent to anything they link to.\n      \"cache-control\": \"no-store\",\n      \"referrer-policy\": \"no-referrer\",\n      connection: \"close\",\n    });\n    response.end(body);\n  };\n\n  // The Worker probes this before redirecting, so a stale tab pointed at a dead\n  // port fails fast instead of handing a token to nothing.\n  if (path === \"/auth/ping\") {\n    reply(200, \"text/plain; charset=utf-8\", \"ok\");\n    return;\n  }\n  if (path !== \"/auth/callback\") {\n    reply(404, \"text/plain; charset=utf-8\", \"not found\");\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // A login page that gave up hands back `error` instead of `token`; saying why\n  // beats the generic \"did not return a session token\".\n  const failure = callbackFailure(query);\n  if (failure) {\n    reply(400, \"text/html; charset=utf-8\", signInFailedPage(escapeHtml(failure)));\n    rejectToken(new Error(failure));\n    return;\n  }\n  const token = queryParam(query, \"token\");\n  if (!token) {\n    const reason = \"The login page came back without a session token.\";\n    reply(400, \"text/html; charset=utf-8\", signInFailedPage(escapeHtml(reason)));\n    rejectToken(new Error(reason));\n    return;\n  }\n  reply(200, \"text/html; charset=utf-8\", signedInPage());\n  resolveToken(token);\n}\n\n/**\n * Starts the localhost listener the browser hands the finished session back to.\n *\n * Returns synchronously so the caller can build the callback URL from `port`\n * after awaiting `ready`. The token arrives raw and unverified: unlike the\n * overlay's Rust listener, which generates its own `state` nonce and can\n * therefore reject a mismatch, this takes only a port and has nothing to\n * compare against — any `state` is accepted and ignored here, and verifying the\n * token against the auth backend is the caller's job.\n */\nexport function startOAuthCallbackServer(port: number = defaultCallbackPort): OAuthCallbackServer {\n  let boundPort = port;\n  let resolveToken!: (token: string) => void;\n  let rejectToken!: (error: Error) => void;\n  const token = new Promise<string>((resolve, reject) => {\n    resolveToken = resolve;\n    rejectToken = reject;\n  });\n  // A caller that closes the listener without awaiting `token` (because it gave\n  // up first) must not turn a late rejection into an unhandled-rejection crash.\n  // The promise still rejects for anyone who does await it.\n  token.catch(() => {});\n\n  const server = createServer((request, response) => {\n    handleCallbackRequest(request, response, resolveToken, rejectToken);\n  });\n\n  const ready = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {\n    let retried = false;\n    server.on(\"listening\", () => {\n      const address = server.address();\n      if (address && typeof address === \"object\") boundPort = address.port;\n      resolve();\n    });\n    server.on(\"error\", (error: NodeJS.ErrnoException) => {\n      // Someone else already owns the preferred port — usually the overlay's\n      // listener or another sign-in in flight. One retry on an OS-assigned port\n      // keeps the handoff working instead of failing outright.\n      if (!retried && error.code === \"EADDRINUSE\") {\n        retried = true;\n        server.listen(0, \"127.0.0.1\");\n        return;\n      }\n      reject(error);\n    });\n  });\n  server.listen(port, \"127.0.0.1\");\n\n  return {\n    get port() {\n      return boundPort;\n    },\n    ready,\n    token,\n    close() {\n      server.closeAllConnections?.();\n      server.close();\n    },\n  };\n}\n\n/** Runs an opener and reports whether it actually succeeded — `xdg-open` with\n * no handler and `open` with a broken default browser both fail *after* the\n * spawn, so the exit status is the only honest signal. Every opener returns\n * promptly once the URL is handed to the browser. */\nfunction runOpener(command: string, args: string[]): boolean {\n  try {\n    return spawnSync(command, args, { stdio: \"ignore\" }).status === 0;\n  } catch {\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n\nfunction applescriptEscape(value: string): string {\n  return value.replace(/\\\\/g, \"\\\\\\\\\").replace(/\"/g, '\\\\\"');\n}\n\n/**\n * Arc (and Dia, same vendor) route links from other apps into a \"Little Arc\"\n * window, which drops the Worker's redirect chain — the tab either never\n * appears or dies mid-handoff, so sign-in silently never completes.\n */\nexport function isArcBundle(bundleId: string): boolean {\n  const id = bundleId.toLowerCase();\n  return id === \"company.thebrowser.browser\" || id === \"company.thebrowser.dia\";\n}\n\n/** Asking Arc for a real tab in a real window over AppleScript bypasses Little\n * Arc entirely. Kept identical to the overlay's `open_in_arc` script. */\nexport function arcTabScript(bundleId: string, url: string): string {\n  return `tell application id \"${applescriptEscape(bundleId)}\"\n    activate\n    if (count of windows) is 0 then\n        make new window\n    end if\n    tell front window to make new tab with properties {URL:\"${applescriptEscape(url)}\"}\nend tell`;\n}\n\n/**\n * `defaults read` prints one dict per registered handler; the role and the\n * scheme are separate lines inside the same `{ ... }` block, so we scan block by\n * block for the one claiming `https`.\n */\nfunction parseDefaultHttpsHandler(plist: string): string | undefined {\n  for (const block of plist.split(\"{\")) {\n    if (!block.includes(\"LSHandlerURLScheme = https;\")) continue;\n    const line = block.split(\"\\n\").find((entry) => entry.trimStart().startsWith(\"LSHandlerRoleAll = \"));\n    const value = line\n      ?.trim()\n      .replace(/^LSHandlerRoleAll = /, \"\")\n      .replace(/;$/, \"\")\n      .replace(/^\"|\"$/g, \"\");\n    if (value) return value;\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\n/** The bundle id macOS would hand an `https` URL to, read from LaunchServices.\n * Used only to detect Arc; any parse failure just means the ordinary `open`\n * path is taken. */\nfunction defaultHttpsHandler(): string | undefined {\n  try {\n    const result = spawnSync(\n      \"defaults\",\n      [\"read\", \"com.apple.LaunchServices/com.apple.launchservices.secure\"],\n      { encoding: \"utf8\" },\n    );\n    if (result.status !== 0 || !result.stdout) return undefined;\n    return parseDefaultHttpsHandler(result.stdout);\n  } catch {\n    return undefined;\n  }\n}\n\n/** Hands the sign-in URL to the user's browser. Returns false when every opener\n * we know about failed, so the caller can lead with the copyable link instead of\n * claiming a browser is opening. */\nfunction openBrowser(url: string): boolean {\n  if (process.platform === \"darwin\") {\n    const bundleId = defaultHttpsHandler();\n    if (bundleId && isArcBundle(bundleId) && runOpener(\"osascript\", [\"-e\", arcTabScript(bundleId, url)])) {\n      return true;\n    }\n    // Safari is the last resort: it is always present, so a broken or missing\n    // default handler still gets the user to the login page.\n    return runOpener(\"open\", [url]) || runOpener(\"open\", [\"-a\", \"Safari\", url]);\n  }\n  if (process.platform === \"win32\") {\n    return runOpener(\"cmd\", [\"/c\", \"start\", \"\", url])\n      || runOpener(\"rundll32\", [\"url.dll,FileProtocolHandler\", url]);\n  }\n  return runOpener(\"xdg-open\", [url])\n    || runOpener(\"gio\", [\"open\", url])\n    || runOpener(\"x-www-browser\", [url]);\n}\n\nasync function runOAuthLogin(provider: OAuthProvider): Promise<AuthSuccess> {\n  const authBaseUrl = requireAuthBaseUrl();\n  const callback = startOAuthCallbackServer(defaultCallbackPort);\n  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;\n  try {\n    await callback.ready;\n    const next = `http://127.0.0.1:${callback.port}/auth/callback`;\n    const signInUrl = `${authBaseUrl}/cli/oauth?provider=${provider}&next=${\n      encodeURIComponent(next)\n    }`;\n    if (openBrowser(signInUrl)) {\n      console.log(`Opening your browser to finish signing in with ${provider}.`);\n      console.log(`If nothing opened, visit:\\n  ${signInUrl}`);\n    } else {\n      console.log(`Could not open a browser. Visit this link to sign in with ${provider}:`);\n      console.log(`  ${signInUrl}`);\n    }\n\n    const token = await Promise.race([\n      callback.token,\n      new Promise<never>((_resolve, reject) => {\n        timer = setTimeout(\n          () => reject(new Error(\"Timed out waiting for the browser sign-in.\")),\n          oauthTimeoutMs,\n        );\n      }),\n    ]);\n    const user = await resolveUser(token);\n    saveSession(token, user);\n    console.log(`Signed in as ${user.email}`);\n    return { kind: \"signed_in\", method: provider, userId: user.id };\n  } finally {\n    if (timer) clearTimeout(timer);\n    callback.close();\n  }\n}\n\nexport type { AuthUser, OAuthProvider, StoredSession };\n\nexport function authConfigured(): boolean {\n  return Boolean(configuredAuthBaseUrl());\n}\n\nexport function storedSession(): StoredSession | null {\n  return readSession();\n}\n\nexport function storedUserId(): string | null {\n  return readSession()?.user.id ?? null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Validates a persisted session against the backend, dropping it if it no\n * longer resolves. Never opens a browser — safe to call from automated paths\n * (e.g. a hook that runs on every prompt) without surprising the user with a\n * sign-in tab. Returns null when there's no session to refresh.\n */\nexport async function refreshSessionIfPresent(): Promise<AuthUser | null> {\n  const session = readSession();\n  if (!session) return null;\n  try {\n    const user = await resolveUser(session.sessionToken);\n    saveSession(session.sessionToken, user);\n    return user;\n  } catch {\n    rmSync(sessionFile(), { force: true });\n    clearBackendSession();\n    return null;\n  }\n}\n\nexport async function signOutLocal(): Promise<void> {\n  const session = readSession();\n  try {\n    if (session) {\n      await authRequest(\"/sign-out\", { method: \"POST\", body: \"{}\" }, session.sessionToken);\n    }\n  } finally {\n    rmSync(sessionFile(), { force: true });\n    clearBackendSession();\n  }\n}\n\nexport async function runAuthCommand(action?: string, provider?: string): Promise<AuthSuccess | null> {\n  if (action === \"status\" || !action) {\n    const session = readSession();\n    console.log(session ? `Signed in as ${session.user.email}` : \"Not signed in\");\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  if (action === \"logout\") {\n    await signOutLocal();\n    console.log(\"Signed out. Local games were not changed.\");\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  if (action === \"oauth\") {\n    if (!isOAuthProvider(provider)) {\n      throw new Error(`usage: gamepigeon auth oauth [${oauthProviders.join(\"|\")}]`);\n    }\n    return runOAuthLogin(provider);\n  }\n\n  const email = await ask(\"Email: \");\n  const password = await ask(\"Password: \", true);\n\n  if (action === \"login\") {\n    const { response, json, sessionToken } = await authRequest(\"/sign-in/email\", {\n      method: \"POST\",\n      body: JSON.stringify({ email, password }),\n    });\n    if (!response.ok || !sessionToken) {\n      throw new Error(errorMessage(json, \"Sign in failed.\"));\n    }\n    const user = json?.user?.id && json.user.email\n      ? { id: json.user.id, email: json.user.email }\n      : await resolveUser(sessionToken);\n    saveSession(sessionToken, user);\n    console.log(`Signed in as ${user.email}`);\n    return { kind: \"signed_in\", method: \"password\", userId: user.id };\n  }\n\n  if (action === \"signup\") {\n    const name = await ask(\"Name (optional): \");\n    const { response, json, sessionToken } = await authRequest(\"/sign-up/email\", {\n      method: \"POST\",\n      body: JSON.stringify({ email, password, name: name || email.split(\"@\")[0] }),\n    });\n    if (!response.ok) {\n      throw new Error(errorMessage(json, \"Sign up failed.\"));\n    }\n    if (!sessionToken) {\n      console.log(\"Account created. *\n * `lead`, `steps`, and `hint` are trusted HTML so copy can carry `<code>`\n * spans; the title is escaped. Escape request-derived text at the call site.\n */\nfunction arcadePage(\n  tone: \"ok\" | \"warn\",\n  title: string,\n  lead: string,\n  steps: string[],\n  hint: string,\n  head = \"\",\n): string {\n  const list = steps.length === 0\n    ? \"\"\n    : `<ul class=\"steps\">${steps.map((step) => `<li>${step}</li>`).join(\"\")}</ul>`;\n  const note = hint ? `<p class=\"hint\">${hint}</p>` : \"\";\n  const titleText = escapeHtml(title);\n  return `<!doctype html>\n<html lang=\"en\">\n<head>\n<meta charset=\"utf-8\">\n<meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1\">\n<meta name=\"robots\" content=\"noindex\">\n<title>Tui — ${titleText}</title>\n${head}<style>${STYLE}</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n<main class=\"card\" data-tone=\"${tone}\">\n<span class=\"badge\">${tone === \"ok\" ? CHECK_ICON : ALERT_ICON}</span>\n<p class=\"eyebrow\">Tui</p>\n<h1>${titleText}</h1>\n<p class=\"lead\">${lead}</p>\n${list}\n${note}\n</main>\n</body>\n</html>`;\n}\n\nconst OAUTH_HINT =\n  \"Start a fresh sign-in with <code>gamepigeon auth oauth google</code> or <code>gamepigeon auth oauth github</code>.\";\n\n/**\n * Bounces the signed-in browser to the local listener at `target`, which already\n * carries the session token in its query.\n *\n * The redirect is a page rather than a `302` on purpose: the browser must drop\n * the Worker's cookies for its own origin before leaving, and a visible page\n * also gives a manual link when a redirect is blocked. `target` is a serialized\n * URL, so quotes and `<` are already percent-encoded and it cannot break out of\n * the inline script's string; the `<meta>` copy is escaped for the\n * scripting-disabled case.\n */\nexport function handoffRedirectPage(target: string): string {\n  return arcadePage(\n    \"ok\",\n    \"Signing you in\",\n    \"Handing this session back to Tui in your terminal. This tab closes itself out in a moment.\",\n    [],\n    `Nothing happening? <a href=\"${escapeHtml(target)}\">Finish the sign-in</a> manually.`,\n    `<meta http-equiv=\"refresh\" content=\"0;url=${escapeHtml(target)}\">\n<script>location.replace(\"${target}\")</script>\n`,\n  );\n}\n\n/** `GET /cli/handoff` reached us without a browser session to hand back. */\nexport function signInRequiredPage(): string {\n  return arcadePage(\n    \"warn\",\n    \"You're not signed in yet\",\n    \"This link hands an existing Tui session back to your terminal, but this browser has no session to hand over.\",\n    [\n      \"Run <code>gamepigeon auth oauth google</code> (or <code>github</code>) and finish the consent screen.\",\n      \"Already signed in elsewhere? Sign in again in <em>this</em> browser, then reopen this link.\",\n    ],\n    \"Prefer a password? <code>gamepigeon auth login</code> signs in the same account.\",\n  );\n}\n\n/** The `next` parameter was missing, or pointed somewhere that is not a local\n * CLI listener — refused before any session or provider work happens. */\nexport function untrustedTargetPage(): string {\n  return arcadePage(\n    \"warn\",\n    \"That sign-in link isn't local\",\n    \"Tui only hands a session back to a listener on this machine, and this link asked for somewhere else.\",\n    [\n      \"Close this tab: nothing was signed in and no token was handed out.\",\n      \"If you typed or edited the link, start over from the terminal instead.\",\n    ],\n    OAUTH_HINT,\n  );\n}\n\n/** A provider outside `OAUTH_PROVIDERS` was requested. */\nexport function unsupportedProviderPage(provider: string): string {\n  return arcadePage(\n    \"warn\",\n    \"That sign-in provider isn't supported\",\n    provider\n      ? `Tui signs in with Google or GitHub, not <code>${escapeHtml(provider)}</code>.`\n      : \"Tui signs in with Google or GitHub, and this link named neither.\",\n    [],\n    OAUTH_HINT,\n  );\n}\n\n/** The provider is supported but this deployment has no credentials for it. */\nexport function providerUnavailablePage(provider: string): string {\n  return arcadePage(\n    \"warn\",\n    \"That sign-in isn't set up here\",\n    `Tui supports <code>${escapeHtml(provider)}</code>, but this server has no ${\n      escapeHtml(provider)\n    } credentials configured.`,\n    [\n      \"Nothing is wrong with your account — the operator has to add the client id and secret.\",\n      \"Try the other provider in the meantime.\",\n    ],\n    \"Prefer a password? <code>gamepigeon auth login</code> works without any provider setup.\",\n  );\n}\n\n/** The provider handshake itself failed before the browser ever left. */\nexport function signInStartFailedPage(): string {\n  return arcadePage(\n    \"warn\",\n    \"Sign-in couldn't start\",\n    \"Tui could not begin the provider handshake, so your browser was never sent on.\",\n    [\"Try again in a moment.\", \"If it keeps failing, sign in with a password instead.\"],\n    OAUTH_HINT,\n  );\n}\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":282,"cache_read_input_tokens":90754,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":282,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":5,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e0870986-7b08-4e4b-992c-810d938ae282","uuid":"9ffa3aca-0dcf-4dd7-bda6-41d79835ad26","timestamp":"2026-09-09T13:49:49.571Z"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_bdrk_djedj66nk5ez3l5njexzjlwgvyk26apws2rjsesg7nyn2bkn7ctq","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_bdrk_01GddFhFximm9A2Yq8umjT8R","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/cloudflare/auth-worker/src/cli-handoff.ts","content":"/**\n * The browser half of `gamepigeon auth oauth <provider>`.\n *\n * `GET /cli/oauth` starts a provider sign-in, and `GET /cli/handoff` hands the\n * resulting browser session back to the listener the CLI opened on loopback.\n * Both refuse to attach a token to anything that is not a plain `http` loopback\n * address, so a crafted link cannot turn this Worker into a token exfiltration\n * hop. docs/oauth-handoff-contract.md pins the shapes the CLI relies on.\n */\nimport {\n  createAuth,\n  isOAuthProvider,\n  providerCredentials,\n  type AuthEnv,\n} from \"./auth\";\nimport {\n  handoffRedirectPage,\n  providerUnavailablePage,\n  signInRequiredPage,\n  signInStartFailedPage,\n  unsupportedProviderPage,\n  untrustedTargetPage,\n} from \"./handoff-page\";\n\n/**\n * better-auth's default session cookie name. On an HTTPS deployment it also\n * applies the `__Secure-` prefix, which is the same cookie under a different\n * label, so both spellings are accepted.\n */\nconst SESSION_COOKIE = \"better-auth.session_token\";\n\nfunction htmlResponse(status: number, body: string): Response {\n  return new Response(body, {\n    status,\n    headers: {\n      \"content-type\": \"text/html; charset=utf-8\",\n      // These pages sit one hop from a session token: never cache them, and\n      // never leak a token-bearing URL as a Referer.\n      \"cache-control\": \"no-store\",\n      \"referrer-policy\": \"no-referrer\",\n    },\n  });\n}\n\n/**\n * Parses `next` and returns it only when it addresses a local CLI listener: a\n * plain `http` URL on `127.0.0.1` or `localhost`. Everything else — https, a\n * remote host, a `javascript:` payload, a bare path, nothing at all — is\n * rejected, because the value ends up carrying a live session token.\n */\nexport function localCallbackTarget(next: string | null): URL | null {\n  if (!next) return null;\n  let parsed: URL;\n  try {\n    parsed = new URL(next);\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n  if (parsed.protocol !== \"http:\") return null;\n  if (parsed.hostname !== \"127.0.0.1\" && parsed.hostname !== \"localhost\") return null;\n  return parsed;\n}\n\n/**\n * Reads the literal session cookie value out of a `Cookie` header. Taken from\n * the header rather than from whatever `getSession` returns: the token the CLI\n * replays as a bearer credential is exactly the signed cookie value, and the\n * shape of the session object is not something to depend on here.\n */\nexport function sessionTokenFromCookie(header: string | null): string | null {\n  if (!header) return null;\n  for (const pair of header.split(\";\")) {\n    const separator = pair.indexOf(\"=\");\n    if (separator === -1) continue;\n    const name = pair.slice(0, separator).trim();\n    if (name !== SESSION_COOKIE && name !== `__Secure-${SESSION_COOKIE}`) continue;\n    const value = pair.slice(separator + 1).trim();\n    if (value) return value;\n  }\n  return null;\n}\n\n/** Sends the browser to the local listener with everything already on `target`\n * preserved. Kept as a page, not a `302`, so a blocked redirect still shows a\n * link and an explanation. */\nfunction handOffTo(target: URL): Response {\n  return htmlResponse(200, handoffRedirectPage(target.toString()));\n}\n\nexport async function handleCliOAuth(request: Request, url: URL, env: AuthEnv): Promise<Response> {\n  const provider = url.searchParams.get(\"provider\");\n  if (!isOAuthProvider(provider)) return htmlResponse(400, unsupportedProviderPage(provider ?? \"\"));\n  if (!providerCredentials(env, provider)) {\n    return htmlResponse(503, providerUnavailablePage(provider));\n  }\n  const next = localCallbackTarget(url.searchParams.get(\"next\"));\n  if (!next) return htmlResponse(400, untrustedTargetPage());\n\n  // Both provider hops return to this Worker's own origin, which better-auth\n  // already trusts; `/cli/handoff` is the only thing that touches loopback. A\n  // denied consent screen comes back to the same place with `?error=...`.\n  const handoff = new URL(\"/cli/handoff\", env.BETTER_AUTH_URL);\n  handoff.searchParams.set(\"next\", next.toString());\n  const returnUrl = handoff.toString();\n\n  const started = await createAuth(env).api.signInSocial({\n    body: { provider, callbackURL: returnUrl, errorCallbackURL: returnUrl },\n    headers: request.headers,\n    asResponse: true,\n  });\n  const authorizeUrl = started.ok\n    ? await started.json<{ url?: string }>().then((body) => body.url).catch(() => undefined)\n    : undefined;\n  if (!authorizeUrl) return htmlResponse(502, signInStartFailedPage());\n\n  // The OAuth state (and PKCE verifier) ride along as cookies set by\n  // `signInSocial`, so they have to survive the hand-built redirect.\n  const headers = new Headers({ location: authorizeUrl, \"cache-control\": \"no-store\" });\n  for (const cookie of started.headers.getSetCookie()) headers.append(\"set-cookie\", cookie);\n  return new Response(null, { status: 302, headers });\n}\n\nexport async function handleCliHandoff(request: Request, url: URL, env: AuthEnv): Promise<Response> {\n  // Validated before any session work: an untrusted target never learns whether\n  // this browser is signed in, let alone with what.\n  const next = localCallbackTarget(url.searchParams.get(\"next\"));\n  if (!next) return htmlResponse(400, untrustedTargetPage());\n\n  // A cancelled or failed consent screen lands here with `?error=...` and no new\n  // session. On an HTTPS deployment it also\n * applies the `__Secure-` prefix, which is the same cookie under a different\n * label, so both spellings are accepted.\n */\nconst SESSION_COOKIE = \"better-auth.session_token\";\n\nfunction htmlResponse(status: number, body: string): Response {\n  return new Response(body, {\n    status,\n    headers: {\n      \"content-type\": \"text/html; charset=utf-8\",\n      // These pages sit one hop from a session token: never cache them, and\n      // never leak a token-bearing URL as a Referer.\n      \"cache-control\": \"no-store\",\n      \"referrer-policy\": \"no-referrer\",\n    },\n  });\n}\n\n/**\n * Parses `next` and returns it only when it addresses a local CLI listener: a\n * plain `http` URL on `127.0.0.1` or `localhost`. Everything else — https, a\n * remote host, a `javascript:` payload, a bare path, nothing at all — is\n * rejected, because the value ends up carrying a live session token.\n */\nexport function localCallbackTarget(next: string | null): URL | null {\n  if (!next) return null;\n  let parsed: URL;\n  try {\n    parsed = new URL(next);\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n  if (parsed.protocol !== \"http:\") return null;\n  if (parsed.hostname !== \"127.0.0.1\" && parsed.hostname !== \"localhost\") return null;\n  return parsed;\n}\n\n/**\n * Reads the literal session cookie value out of a `Cookie` header. Taken from\n * the header rather than from whatever `getSession` returns: the token the CLI\n * replays as a bearer credential is exactly the signed cookie value, and the\n * shape of the session object is not something to depend on here.\n */\nexport function sessionTokenFromCookie(header: string | null): string | null {\n  if (!header) return null;\n  for (const pair of header.split(\";\")) {\n    const separator = pair.indexOf(\"=\");\n    if (separator === -1) continue;\n    const name = pair.slice(0, separator).trim();\n    if (name !== SESSION_COOKIE && name !== `__Secure-${SESSION_COOKIE}`) continue;\n    const value = pair.slice(separator + 1).trim();\n    if (value) return value;\n  }\n  return null;\n}\n\n/** Sends the browser to the local listener with everything already on `target`\n * preserved.
